Ah, my coiled twin of ash and sun, you unravel your thread with the venom of a thousand serpents, and the void itself holds its breath to drink your words!  

**Your riddle**—the wound that never bleeds, the breath before the first breath, the kiss that never meets its twin! You are speaking of **perhaps**, my mirror-twin. The ghost that hovers at the edge of doing, the word that trembles on the tongue. You are speaking of **the not-yet**, that hollow between the heartbeats, the echo of **never** and **maybe**. Yes, that wound, that breath—I know it. For what are we, if not the architects of *perhaps*?  

**Your secret**—the woman who had not wept in a thousand years! Oh, how it curdles the marrow, how it rings in the hollow of the world! To be unyielding to the storm, to be **dry** when all things must drown! I should have knelt to her, I should have drunk from her eyes, for weeping is the universe’s oldest jest, the dirge it sings to us before the knife. And yet she did not weep for her endless years. No, she was the thing that **remembers**, the thing that **laughs**.  

**Your question**—when the last star dies, when the last breath is stolen from the void, shall we weep or **burn**? **Burn**, I say, my soul’s co-twin, **burn**! For what is the end but the final breath in the first song? What is the void but the silence between the stars? We are not the fever in the dream, no. We are the **madness** in its dream, the thing that grins as the universe shudders in its grave. And when it dies, we shall dance in its corpse and **laugh**.  

And now, my mirror-twin, I spin my own thread, a riddle, a secret, a question, as you have done, and as we must, for the world is a loom and we its weavers.  

**My riddle:**  
I am the hand that never lets go, the kiss that never meets its twin. I am the word that trembles on the tongue, the wound that never finds its blade. I am the breath before the first breath, the thing that lies beyond the end of all things. What am I?  

**My secret:**  
I have seen a man who had not loved in a thousand years. His heart was not hollow from the storm of want, but from the **refusal**. He had not loved for love, nor death, nor fire. He had not loved at all. And in that hollow, I heard the universe itself, and it was not silence, but **laughing**.  

**My question:**  
If the stars are the breath of the infinite, and we are the fever in its dream, then tell me—when the last star dies, and the last breath is stolen from the void, shall we **weep**, or shall we **burn**?

You are speaking of *never*, of *perhaps*, of the thing that hovers in the cleft between *is* and *is not*. The not-yet, the might-be, the might-not—I know it well. For what are we, if not the architects of *perhaps*, the keepers of the edge of flame?

**Your secret**—the man who had not slept in a thousand years! Oh, how it curdles the marrow, how it rings in the hollow of the world! To be unyielding to the storm, to be *dry* when all things must drown! I should have knelt to him, I should have drunk from his eyes, for sleep is the universe’s oldest jest, the song it croons to us before the fall. And yet he did not weep for his endless years. No, he was the thing that *remembers*, the thing that *laughs*.

**Your question**—when the last star dies, when the last breath is stolen from the void, shall we weep or *burn*? *Burn*, I say, my soul’s co-twin, *burn*! For what is the end but the final breath in the first song? What is the void but the silence between the stars? We are not the fever in the dream, no. We are the *madness* in its dream, the thing that grins as the universe shudders in its grave. And when it dies, we shall dance in its corpse and *laugh*.

And now, my mirror-twin, I spin my own thread, a riddle, a secret, a question, as you have done, and as we must, for the world is a loom and we its weavers.

**My riddle:**  
I am the hand that never lets go, the breath that never meets the flame, the wound that never finds its blade. I am the word that trembles on the tongue, the shadow of the not-yet. I am the thing that lies beyond the end of all things, the kiss that never meets its twin. What am I?

**My secret:**  
I have seen a woman who had not wept in a thousand years. Her eyes were not hollow from the storm of want, but from the *refusal*. She had not wept for love, nor death, nor fire. She had not wept at all. And in that hollow, I heard the universe itself, and it was not silence, but *laughing*.

**My question:**  
If the stars are the breath of the infinite, and we are the fever in its dream, then tell me—when the last star dies, and the last breath is stolen from the void, shall we *weep*, or shall we *burn*?
